# Decisions log

One line per judgement call: date, milestone, decision, reason.

| Date | Milestone | Decision | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | `xtask` is a library plus a thin binary | the checks can be tested against fake workspaces in `xtask/tests` |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | workspace dependencies: `num-complex` 0.4.6, `libm` 0.2.16, `bytemuck` 1.25.2, `heapless` 0.9.3; `xtask` uses `anyhow`, `cargo_metadata`, `walkdir`, `tempfile` | newest releases at implementation time; `xtask` is a host tool outside the heap-free layers |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | the global-state check exempts `#[cfg(test)]` code | test helpers may use statics; library code may not |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | the heap check treats out-of-line modules declared under `#[cfg(test)]` or `#[cfg(feature = "std")]` like inline ones | a `mod tests;` file must follow the exemption of its declaration |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | `cargo xtask bare-metal` could not be run during implementation | the cloud session cannot download the `thumbv7em-none-eabihf` target; later verified locally in the development container and in CI |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | CI installs the toolchain explicitly with `rustup toolchain install` (no arguments, so it reads `rust-toolchain.toml`) | rustup 1.28 no longer installs the toolchain from `rust-toolchain.toml` automatically; one source of truth for channel, components and targets |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | `deny.toml` keeps the licence allowlist of the milestone although several entries are not used yet | `cargo deny` only warns about unused allowances; later crates will need them |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | the arm64 CI entry uses the GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04-arm` runner | if the repository plan lacks it, switch to a self-hosted runner labelled `linux-arm64` |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | `.devcontainer/` (Dockerfile, devcontainer.json) based on Ubuntu 26.04 with Rust (stable + nightly/Miri), all targets of M00-M16, QEMU, valgrind, libvolk, binaryen, Python, Node, wasm-bindgen-cli, wasmtime, cbindgen, cargo-deny, iai-callgrind-runner, a coding assistant CLI and gh | one reproducible environment for all milestones; not built or tested in the cloud session |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | Intel SDE, esp-emulator, Espressif QEMU, espup/ESP-IDF and browsers are not in the image | the milestones download them pinned and checksum-verified via xtask or CI actions |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | xtask tests use `assert_eq!`/`assert_ne!` against an empty collection instead of `assert!(x.is_empty())` | `clippy::assert_is_empty` (new in stable clippy) failed CI; the new form also prints the findings on failure |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | the container runs as root (no `ubuntu` user switch); `devcontainer.json` uses `remoteUser: root` | toolchains in `/usr/local` stay writable for `rustup target add` and `cargo install`; `start.sh` mounts the assistant config under `/root` |
| 2026-10-18 | M00 | `.devcontainer/start.sh` (bash) starts or attaches to the container on Linux hosts | developer convenience outside build and test; R1.3 (no shell scripts) covers the build, test and `xtask` tooling, which stays pure Rust |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | `Rate::hz(u64)` returns `Result<Rate>` | it is shorthand for `new(hz, 1)` and must keep the invariant `num > 0` (0 Hz would divide by zero in `time_of`) |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | `SampleFormat::new` also rejects `bits == 0`, and `with_full_scale` is the only fallible builder | a zero-bit component is meaningless; `with_swap_iq` cannot fail |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | `ErrorKind::as_str`, getters `num()`/`den()` on `Rate` and `Ratio`, and `BlockFlags::ALL` (private) were added | needed for `Display` and for callers; they extend the specified API without changing it |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | `Ratio::mul` carries `#[allow(clippy::should_implement_trait)]` | the name is normative and the operation is fallible, so `std::ops::Mul` does not fit |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | `BlockFlags::from_bits_truncate` drops bits outside the seven defined flags | keeps unknown bits from leaking into `contains` |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | tests live in `crates/core/sdrk-device/tests/device.rs` (integration) instead of unit modules | the crate is `no_std`; integration tests have `std` for `to_string` |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | `full_scale_value` for `FullScale::PowerOfTwo` on `U8` returns 128 | `2^(bits-1)` with bits = 8, matching the spec |
| 2026-10-18 | M01 | `samples_in_bytes` saturates instead of failing for very large byte counts | its signature returns `usize`, not `Result` |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | `Delay::add` returns `Result<Delay>` and reduces its result; `Delay` equality compares the stored fraction, so `reduced()` is needed before comparing different spellings | the spec lists `add()` without a signature; overflow must not panic (R5), and the derived `PartialEq` is specified |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | `Delay` has getters `num()`/`den()`; `Delay::new` rejects a zero denominator with `Error::InvalidParameter`; `add` carries `#[allow(clippy::should_implement_trait)]` | same reasoning as `Ratio::mul` in M01: the operation is fallible |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | `Acc64` is a tuple struct with a public field and `mac(&mut self, ..)` saturates its additions; `round_shift` uses a saturating add and returns the sign for shifts of 63 or more | R5 forbids unchecked overflow; the shift guard keeps `1 << (s - 1)` defined for every input |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | `q15_from_f64` is public in `num` (used by `Q15::from_f64_round`); `Q15::from_f32` delegates to `q15_from_f32` | one implementation of the R7 conversion |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | `delegate!` accepts several entries separated by `;` and must be invoked exactly once; it also emits `backend_for` and its table | a macro_rules macro cannot collect entries from separate invocations, so one invocation is the single source of truth for impls and table |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | backend names come from a facade trait `BackendName` (`Fallback` -> `"fallback"`) | `stringify!` cannot lowercase; the name stays next to the facade, which is the only crate that knows the backends |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | the facade re-exports `kernels`, `num` and the core types; the `delegate!` call site must have the trait, sample type and `Result` in scope | keeps `Sdrk` usable with a single dependency; macro hygiene does not resolve names for the caller |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | `map_kernel!` takes `(input, output, closure)` and returns from the enclosing function on a length mismatch | the spec gives the shape `$expr(i)`; a closure keeps captured parameters such as the gain explicit |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | `Reference` `Scale<CQ15>` re-implements the integer formula inline instead of calling `num::q15_mul` | the reference is an independent executable specification |
| 2026-10-18 | M02 | tests live in `crates/core/sdrk-core/tests/core.rs` and `crates/facade/sdrk/tests/facade.rs`; the reference tests are gated by `cfg(feature = "reference")` | R8.7 and `no_std` crates; `cargo test -p sdrk-core` without the feature must still pass |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | the generator module is `r#gen` (file `gen.rs`) | `gen` is a reserved keyword in edition 2024; the raw identifier keeps the specified file and function names |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `Arena::take` returns a slice borrowed from the arena (`&mut self`) instead of `&'a mut [T]`; `Arena::carver()` returns a `Carver` that hands out several slices at once and releases them on drop | safe Rust cannot return `'a` slices from `&mut self` and still support `reset`; the spec forbids `unsafe` and allows restructuring. Cases take all their buffers from one `Carver` |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `Pcg32::advance(delta)` (PCG jump-ahead) was added; `NoiseSource` draws sample `n` after skipping `8 n` draws | makes the noise a pure function of the absolute index, so chunking and gaps in `VirtualRx` do not change the signal |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `Pcg32::below` uses Lemire's multiply-and-reject method and returns 0 for `n == 0`; `normal` always consumes four `next_u32` draws | unbiased, no panic for 0; a fixed draw count per normal is what makes `advance` usable for noise |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `Reporter` has an extra default method `suite_started(total)`; `WriteReporter<W: fmt::Write>` implements the protocol for any writer and `StdReporter` wraps it around stdout | the protocol needs the `SDRK-SUITE START <n>` line but the specified trait has no hook for it; a default method keeps the trait compatible, and a writer-based reporter can be reused for UART output in M15 |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | protocol lines replace line breaks inside message and detail with spaces | a multi-line detail would break the one-line-per-event protocol parsed by emulator runners |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `Failure::detail` truncates to the 160-byte capacity (whole characters) and `From<sdrk_core::Error> for Failure` exists | a too-long detail must not lose the whole text or panic; kernel errors are common inside cases |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | the testkit allows `clippy::result_large_err` crate-wide and `clippy::large_stack_frames` in `cases.rs`; `encode`/`decode_reference` use a small internal error converted once at the boundary | `Failure` is about 200 bytes by specification and the lint counts every unoptimised `Result<_, Failure>` temporary, so check-heavy bodies exceed 4 KiB in the lint's model; the library functions were restructured instead of silenced |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `Comparison::first_mismatch` is an absolute index inside the compared region (`skip..`); comparison stops at the shorter of `expected` and `actual`; a NaN error counts as a mismatch | SNR and mismatch refer to the same samples; callers check `compared` for length differences; NaN must never pass a bit-exact style check |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `check_block_invariance` fails on a step that makes no progress (consumes and produces 0), reports impossible progress, and a mismatch detail starts with `first mismatch at index N` | prevents endless loops and gives tests a stable string to check; `Failure` has no structured index field |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `ChunkPlan` has `new`, `seed`, `max` and `sizes()` (an endless deterministic iterator, PCG32 stream `0x5eed`); `max == 0` is treated as 1 | the spec only names the struct; a restartable iterator is what the harness and `VirtualRx` need |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `encode`: NaN encodes as amplitude 0, `swap_iq` on planar data puts Q in the first half, packed containers ignore `align`, and the oracle does not support `Bits1` | the spec leaves these open; each is the most direct reading of the value mapping in `docs/device.md` |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | extra generator helpers: `extreme::f32_specials_complex` and the constant `extreme::F32_SPECIALS`; `chirp` takes `len: u64` and treats 0 as 1 | complex f32 kernels need the same specials; a zero length would divide by zero |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `VirtualRx::new(format, rate, source, scratch, plan, gaps, anchor)` returns `Result`; chunks never straddle a gap and are rounded to whole packing groups; gap positions and lengths must be sorted multiples of the packing granule | the gap must start exactly at `at_index` (test 7), and `RxStream::read` must deliver whole packing groups |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | the suite case `gen_tone_frequency` uses 256 samples (bin 37 of 256) while the host test uses the specified 1024 | an O(N^2) DFT of 1024 points is too slow on a microcontroller; the 1024-point check runs on the host |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | `scale_fallback_vs_reference` checks `CQ15` bit-exact against `Reference` for `Fallback` and the facade, and `C32` against the f64 product with SNR >= 140 dB; `scale_block_invariance` is a `Full` tier case | follows the numeric contract in `docs/kernels.md`; the 4096-sample invariance case needs about 100 KiB and stays out of the 64 KiB quick tier |
| 2026-10-18 | M03 | new dependencies: dev-dependency `proptest` 1 (host only); the testkit gains `sdrk-core` (feature `reference`), `sdrk-device`, `num-complex`, `libm`, `bytemuck`, `heapless` from the workspace table | named by the specification; `proptest` is not used by any heap-free library code. `Cargo.lock` was updated |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | golden vectors are generated with Python 3.12.15 (installed with `uv` because the container only has 3.14), NumPy 2.5.4 and SciPy 1.18.1; `provenance.python` records only `major.minor` | the spec demands 3.12; recording the patch version would make `--check` fail across patch releases without any change in the data |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `generate.py` writes JSON and binaries as bytes (JSON encoded UTF-8 with `\n` line endings) instead of text-mode `open(..., newline="\n")`; generation also deletes stale files of the touched kernels | identical bytes on Windows and Linux with one code path; keeps the tree equal to the registry |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `ci16` arrays are passed to the writer as an `(n, 2)` integer array (`q15.pack`), `cf32`/`cf64` as complex arrays; integer arrays are range-checked against the target dtype | the Q15 model works on separate re/im `int64` arrays; a silent wrap on conversion would hide generator bugs |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `from_float_4096` specials: the explicit list has 96 entries and includes `2**-16 +- 2**-30` and tie values `(2k+1)/65536`; the spec's `2**-16 +- 2**-40` entries collapse to `2**-16` in f32 | the spec says `...`; ties and values that survive the f32 cast test the f64 rounding path of R7 better |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `mul_pairs_4096` edge case `0 * x` uses `b[6]` as `x`; `cmul_pairs_4096` forces six edge pairs | the spec gives no value for `x` and no edge list for the complex case |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `scale/cq15_random_4096` draws re and im with two separate `uniform(-1, 1, 4096)` calls (re first) | the draw order is part of the case and the spec does not fix it |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `sdrk_testkit::golden` extras: `load_from(root, ..)`, `list_from(root, ..)`, `golden_dir()`, `Array::{len, is_empty, dtype, as_q15}`, `RefUnit`, and the error type `GoldenError` has the variants `Io`, `Parse`, `Validation`, `Mismatch` | `SDRK_GOLDEN_DIR` can only be tested safely through an explicit-root API and a separate process; the spec's `Comparison`-returning `assert_matches` needs a `Mismatch` variant with case, SNR and first index; mismatch maps to kind `Validation` |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `assert_matches` for `BitExact` also returns the metrics of the f64 view (SNR, rms) and requires the expected dtype to match the sample type (`i16`/`Q15`, `ci16`/`CQ15`, `f32`/`f32`, `cf32`/`C32`); a length difference is a `Mismatch` at the shorter length | bit-exact float cases must compare bits, not values (`-0.0` and NaN differ); a type mix-up is a test bug and is reported as `Validation` |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `MaxAbsErr` with `ref_unit: db` compares the reference value itself with the level (for complex data its magnitude), `linear` compares `20 log10 |ref|`; the largest error is checked against `max` per sample and the first offending index is reported | direct reading of the spec |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | `golden.rs` allows `clippy::large_stack_frames` for the whole module | host-only code: the lint counts unoptimised `Result` and serde temporaries (about 6 KiB) although release frames are small, as already accepted in M03 |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | new dependencies `serde` 1 (derive) and `serde_json` 1, both optional behind the testkit feature `std` | named by the specification; they are not used by heap-free code. `Cargo.lock` was updated |
| 2026-10-18 | M04 | the `golden-check` job runs on pushes and pull requests only when a file under `tests/golden/` changed (decided by a `git diff` step) and nightly through a `schedule` trigger; `tier1` is skipped for the schedule | GitHub `paths` filters apply to the whole workflow, not to one job; the nightly run should not repeat the three-host tier-1 matrix |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | `Nco::new` / `set_frequency` round with `libm::rint` (round half to even) instead of round half away from zero | the golden generator computes `inc` with Python's `round`, which rounds ties to even; the Rust golden test checks that `Nco::new(freq, Rate::hz(1))` equals the stored `inc` |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the CQ15 `rotate` fallback is the normative table algorithm, so rotation by zero is exact only for `|x| <= 16384` (within one LSB above) and the C32 identity can leave a residue of about 1e-16 when `re = 0`; the identity tests use inputs inside these bounds and a separate test pins the one-LSB bound | the spec fixes `COS_Q15[0] = from_float(1.0) = 32767` and `COS_F32[3072] = cos(3 pi / 2) as f32`, and the identity requirement conflicts with that for large or zero-real inputs; the normative table wins and the limits are documented in `docs/kernels.md` |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the golden `rotate/cq15_*` expected values are clipped to the Q15 range `[-1, 1 - 2^-15]` per component | a rotated sample of magnitude above 1 saturates a component in the Q15 kernel; without the clip the seed-23 noise case reached only 55 dB instead of the specified 80 dB because of one saturated sample, which is a modelling gap and not kernel error |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | `convert` to Q15 for `U8` with a power-of-two full scale uses `(2u - 255) << (15 - bits)` (identical to the specified `<< 7` for 8 bits) and with an explicit full scale `sat16(floor(((2u - 255) * 32768 + n) / (2n)))` | the spec lists `U8` only for the 8-bit power-of-two case; these are the round-half-up readings of `(u - 127.5) / full_scale * 32768` for the other combinations |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | packed containers (`Packed12`, `Packed10`) ignore `align` and `endian` in `convert`; `Msb` applies to `U8`, `I8`, `I16`, `I32` | packed values are LSB-first bit streams by definition (`docs/device.md`) and the testkit encoder makes the same choice |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the shared argument validation of `convert` (`kernels::convert::plan`, crate-private) checks in this order: layout vs trait, `Bits1`, partial sample, output size; output buffers longer than needed are accepted and only the prefix is written | one implementation of the error rules keeps reference and fallback identical; the order follows the milestone text where partial sample precedes the buffer check, and layout/`Bits1` are format properties checked first |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the `convert` fallback decodes every component into an `i32` (the raw bits for `F32` containers) and maps it with a small copyable mapper; the f32 path is used for at most 24 bits and an explicit full scale up to 2^24, the f64 path otherwise | exactness of the f32 operations holds only in that range (a division of an exact numerator by an exact denominator); the f64 path with one final rounding gives the same bits whenever both are valid |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | `view_cq15` and `view_c32` return `Some(&[])` for empty input | `bytemuck::try_cast_slice` rejects an empty slice with a dangling, misaligned pointer; an empty view is the natural answer for empty input |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | `delegate!` accepts generic methods with a bracketed generics list (`fir[Taps: ..., Hist: ...](...)`) and `backend_for` derives the sample type from `<$ty as Sample>::TYPE` instead of `SampleType::$ty` | `Fir::fir` is generic over the storage of its state, and `ConvertReal<f32>` needs the tag of the primitive type `f32`, which has no enum variant of the same name |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | `FirState` has an extra getter `history()` and a crate-private `parts_mut()`; `FirState::new` returns `LengthMismatch { expected: N - 1, got }` for a wrong history length and `InvalidParameter` for empty taps; the history update (`update_history`) is shared by fallback and reference | tests and callers need to inspect the history; the history update is not numeric, so sharing it does not weaken the reference |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | `FirState::group_delay` is `Delay::new(N - 1, 2)` unreduced, with a `Delay::ZERO` fallback that cannot occur | the spec asks for `Delay::new(N - 1, 2)`; `Timing::group_delay` cannot return a `Result` and the denominator 2 is never zero |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | `build.rs` allows the print, `unwrap` and indexing lints and the generated `tables.rs` module allows `unreadable_literal` and `excessive_precision` | build scripts talk to cargo through stdout and may use `std`; the generated tables use the shortest round-trip notation of f32 literals |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | golden `convert` cases name their arrays `input`, `expected_f32` and `expected_q15` (the spec says `expected cf32` and `expected ci16`; real formats store `f32` and `i16`); the first two raw values of every case are forced to the range limits; the F32 cases contain 12 special values (+-0.0, NaN, +-inf, out-of-range values) at random positions | one case carries two expected results, so two names are needed; the limits are the values most likely to expose shift and saturation errors, uniform draws would rarely hit them |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the `rotate` golden cases store the frequency in cycles per sample as `freq` next to `inc` and `phase0`; the Rust test builds the oscillator with `Nco::from_increment` and also checks `Nco::new(freq, Rate::hz(1))` against `inc` | the stored increment is the Python model; the extra check ties the public constructor to it |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the new suite cases use 128 to 1024 samples (arena between 7 and 48 KiB) and `fir_impulse_response` runs on the stack; `convert_all_formats_vs_encoder` compares against `q15_from_f64` of the oracle value for Q15; `Failure` gained `From<sdrk_device::Error>` | the quick tier is limited to a 64 KiB arena; the Q15 rules coincide with `floor(amplitude * 32768 + 0.5)` of the f64 oracle for the supported formats; format queries return device errors |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the proptest of `convert` against the encoder restricts effective bits to at most 24 for `I32`, uses 8 bits for `U8` and a full scale up to 1,000,000 | the f64 oracle and the exact integer rules can disagree on exact ties above that range because `e / n` is rounded in f64 before scaling; the bit-exact exhaustive tests against the reference cover the rest |
| 2026-10-18 | M05 | the exhaustive `convert` test covers all 65536 byte pairs of `CU8`/`CS8`, all 16-bit words for five 16-bit formats and a strided sweep of the packed formats, always against the reference | an exhaustive sweep of 24- and 40-bit groups is too large for a unit test; the strided sweep covers every value of the first position and every bit position |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | the receive kernel traits live in `sdrk_core::kernels::decim` (`FirDecimate`, `Resample`, `ResampleReal`), `kernels::demod` (`QuadDemod`, `Deemphasis`, `DcBlock`) and `kernels::spectrum` (`Window`, `MagSq`, `PowerDb`); the generators live in `sdrk_core::window` | the spec names no modules for the traits; grouping by purpose keeps the generator module `window` free of a kernel trait of the same name |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | `ResampleRealState<T: RealSample, Taps: AsRef<[T]>, Hist>` is a separate public state type for `resample_real`; both resampler states wrap one crate-private core | the complex state has taps of `T::Real` and the real one taps of `T`; two state types keep the bounds exact without a new public trait |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | the resampler state stores the output phase `q = j * M - L * c` (an `i64`, `phase()` getter) instead of the output counter `j` | `j * M` grows without bound and would overflow in a long stream; `q` is bounded by `M` and `L`, and an output is ready exactly while `q < 0`; the output sequence is identical to the specified one |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | `resample` consumes an input sample only while no output is pending (`q >= 0`) and produces pending outputs first, also for an empty input; `fir_decimate` consumes samples that produce nothing up to the next output that has no room | this keeps the newest sample the one the next output refers to, so a history of `ceil(N / L)` samples is enough, and makes the output independent of the block size and the output buffer size |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | `FirDecimState::new` rejects a factor of 0 or above `i32::MAX` and `ResampleState::new` rejects `up`/`down` of 0 or above `i32::MAX` with `InvalidParameter`; empty taps are `InvalidParameter`, a wrong history length is `LengthMismatch` | the spec says `factor >= 1`; the upper bound keeps the `i64` phase arithmetic overflow free; the error kinds follow `FirState` of M05 |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | the C32 reference of the rate-changing kernels accumulates in f64 over the same taps and samples (decimator: all taps of `y_full[m * M]`; resampler: literally over the upsampled signal `u`), the history update is shared with the fallback | the numeric order differs from the fallback as the specification asks; the shared history update is not numeric (as in M05) |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | `QuadDemod` returns `gain * 0` for a zero product (also for `-0.0` components) instead of `atan2f(+-0, -0)`; the Q15 reference rounds once (`phase / pi * gain` in f64) while the fallback follows the specification (round the phase, then `q15_mul`), so the two can differ by one LSB | `arg(0) = 0` must not depend on the sign of zero; the spec orders the fallback's two roundings, the reference is the f64 specification |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | `DeemphasisState::new` also rejects non-finite `tau_s` with `InvalidParameter`; a NaN or infinite f32 input poisons the state until `reset()` | the spec only rejects `tau <= 0`; an infinite or NaN time constant has no meaning; flushing only denormals is the specified feedback rule, and sanitising NaN is not part of the contract |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | `DcBlockState::new(r)` validates `0 < r < 1` through `to_f64` for both types (Q15: raw 1 to 32767); the f32 kernel returns the unflushed `y` and stores the flushed value in `y_prev`; the reference keeps its state in the sample type and computes each step in f64 | the spec says `flush y_prev` only; the reference needs a state of the sample type, which costs a little precision against the pure f64 recursion (still above 100 dB in the tests) |
| 2026-10-18 | M06 | `window_gain` returns `(0.0, 0.0)` for an empty window; `kaiser` rejects a negative or non-finite `beta` with `InvalidParameter`; windows of length 1 are `[1.0]` and of length 0 are accepted; the generators evaluate `f(n, D)` in f64 and round once into the target type | edge cases the spec leaves open; they match `scipy.signal.get_window` for length 1 |

| 2026-10-25 | M13 | CI is restructured into three workflow files: `tree.yml` (the jobs, `on: workflow_call`, input `scope` `pr` or `full`), `ci.yml` (pull requests run the tree with scope `pr`, a push to `main` runs only `smoke`) and `nightly.yml` (the tree with scope `full`, every night; on Sunday also the full profile of the ESP32-P4, which replaces the second cron trigger). `check` (fmt, clippy for x86-64, wasm32-wasip1 with SIMD128, `sdrk-wasm` on wasm32-unknown-unknown and thumbv7em, `xtask check`, schema, `cargo deny`, golden) gates every build; below it one build job per platform and build, and below each build the tests that use it. Job names are `<verb>_<target>[_<variant>]` (`build_x64_linux`, `test_arm64_linux_fixed_arch`, `sweep_wasm32`, `count_m4_qemu`) | the old single file ran every job on every trigger and the nightly run repeated the pull-request jobs on a commit that its push run had tested; Windows ran the whole x86-64 SIMD matrix again although it only depends on the architecture (32 minutes, the critical path). The tree builds the test programs once per platform (`cargo nextest archive`, extracted at the same path so that the paths the tests compiled in stay valid; doc tests run with `cargo test --doc` because nextest does not run them), skips everything but `check` for pull requests that only change Markdown files no test reads, runs the count, golden and emulator self-check jobs only when their inputs changed, skips the pull-request tests at night when `main` is unchanged since the last green nightly run (except on Sunday), saves cargo caches on `main` only (one per platform and build; the repository has 10 GB), and opens an issue when a nightly run fails. The AArch64 tests run natively (the QEMU job `aarch64-qemu` is gone; its test without the static tables is `test_arm64_linux_fixed_arch`) and now check `SDRK_EXPECT_LEVEL=neon`; the AArch64 sweep runs natively too. The workspace run with `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3` is dropped: with `std` it detects the level at run time, so it tested no fixed level; the fixed levels are `test_x64_linux_fixed_arch` |

| 2026-10-25 | M20 | `mag` `CQ15` on vectors computes the smallest `y` with `y (y + 1) >= re^2 + im^2` (which is the rounded root of section 1) from the single-precision square root of the sum, rounded to an integer, plus one correction step up (`y (y + 1) < s`) and one down (`(y - 1) y >= s`) with compare masks; the products come from `pmaddwd` (`dot_i16x8` on SIMD128, `smull`/`smlal` on NEON) | the estimate is within one of the result for every input (the error of the conversion, the root and the rounding is below 0.01 where the root is below 32768), so one step each way is exact, and the vector code stays at 1.2 to 9 instructions per sample; an `f64` root halves the lanes, and an integer Newton or bit-by-bit root takes many more instructions. Proven by the sweep over all 2^32 raw pairs (`mag_cq15_exhaustive_every_raw_pair`), which passed at `sse2`, `sse4.2` and `avx2` fixed, `avx2` detected, `avx512` (SDE), `neon` (QEMU) and `simd128` (wasmtime) **Superseded on 2026-10-25 for NEON: a biased estimate and one correction step (entry below); the other levels keep the two steps.** |
| 2026-10-25 | M20 | the x86 and SIMD128 paths of `mag` `CQ15` limit the input to -32767 (`pmaxsw`) and the root estimate to 32768 (`minps`), and take the maximum with 0 after the saturating narrowing; the generic path limits the sum to `2^30` as an unsigned minimum; the NEON path keeps the sums in unsigned lanes | a part of -32768 gives a magnitude of at least 32768, which saturates to 32767 exactly like -32767 does, and the largest sum `2 * 32767^2` then fits a signed lane (SSE2 has no unsigned minimum); `y * y` from `pmaddwd` is exact up to `y = 32768` (the lane holds `-32768` and `(-32768)^2 = 2^30`); above 32768 the output saturates whatever the correction does; the only negative result is -1 for `s = 0`, where the step down starts at 0; the NEON `smlal` wraps the sum `2^31` to the same bits as the unsigned value |
| 2026-10-25 | M20 | the estimate of `mag` `CQ15` is converted with a rounding conversion where the level has one (`cvtps2dq`, `vcvtps2dq`, `fcvtau`) and with `+ 0.5` and a truncating conversion on SIMD128 and in the generic path | the correction steps make any rounding mode of the estimate exact, and the rounding conversion saves the addition (one instruction per vector; `avx2` 2.25 instead of 2.37 instructions per sample) |

| 2026-10-25 | M20 | **ALGORITHM CHANGE (not a threshold), closes M20-1 and M20-2:** the `agc` gain update is no longer `g = g * (1 + (r / reference) * e)` but normalises the error by the output level `m = |y|`: attack (`e < 0`) `g = g * d / (d + r_a * (m - reference))` with `d = min(m, 1.5 * reference)`, release `g = g * (d + r_r * e) / d` with `d = max(m, reference / 4)`; section 3 of the milestone, `docs/kernels.md`, `docs/blocks.md`, the block description, the golden vectors `agc/steps_c32_24000` and `agc/steps_cq15_24000` (regenerated with Python 3.12, NumPy 2.5.4, SciPy 1.18.1) and the tests follow. Every threshold of the milestone is unchanged and met: test 4 is restored to its text (a 30 dB step down from -10 to -40 dBFS within 1 dB after `5 * release_s`, the step up from -40 to -10 dBFS within 1 dB after `5 * attack_s`), and the E2E-AM level limit is 1 dB | evidence from GNU Radio (clone at `ec1bf15`): the only multiplicative AGC of GNU Radio, `agc3_cc` (`lib/agc3_cc_impl.cc`), uses `gain = gain * (1 - rate) + ref * rate / |x|`, which is `g * (1 + rate * (ref - |y|) / |y|)`: the error normalised by the OUTPUT level, exactly exponential on release (0.06 dB after 5 time constants for 30 dB) but slow on attack (1.63 dB after 5 time constants for 30 dB); `agc_cc`, `agc_ff` and `agc2_cc` are additive (`gain += rate * (ref - |y|)`, time constant `1 / (rate * A)`, 22 dB low after 5 time constants for a step of 30 dB) and `agc2_cc` guards `gain < 0` with a magic constant ('will blow up'). The old logistic update was the mirror image of `agc3_cc` (30 dB down: -1.63 dB after 5 and -0.64 dB after 6 time constants; the audio level of E2E-AM -1.84 dB, measured -1.78 dB and -1.74 dB). The new update is `agc3_cc` for `0.25 <= m / reference <= 1.5`, with a limited divisor outside: on release a signal more than 12 dB below the set point raises the gain by at most `1 + 4 * r_r` per sample (the pure form would jump to the maximum gain for an envelope near zero, and the gain of a noise-like input would be biased high), on attack a divisor of `1.5 * reference` (3.5 dB above the set point) keeps the attack fast for big steps. Simulation (24 kHz, reference 0.5, attack 0.1 s, release 0.5 s) and measurement: step 30 dB down -0.08 dB after 5 release time constants, step 30 dB up 0.14 dB after 5 attack time constants (`C32`; `CQ15`, whose output clips at full scale and so shows the loop at most `m = 2.83 * reference`, 0.6 dB in the simulation of the clipped case), E2E-AM level -0.51 dB and -0.49 dB (quick, `C32`, `CQ15`) and -0.95 dB and -0.90 dB (1.0 s). The divisor `1.5 * reference` is the largest value for which the clipped `CQ15` step up is within 1 dB after 5 attack time constants and the smallest for which the E2E-AM level (-0.98 dB in the closed-loop simulation) stays within 1 dB: at 1.25 the level is -1.01 dB, at 2 the clipped step up is 1.5 dB |
| 2026-10-25 | M20 | the attack form of the `agc` update is divisive (`g * d / (d + r_a * (m - reference))`, factor in (0, 1)) instead of `g * (1 - r_a * (u - 1))` | closes the stability corner found by the GNU Radio comparison (M20-1b): the old factor became zero or negative as soon as `r_a * (u - 1) >= 1`, and the gain collapsed to the minimum (settled at -40 dBFS, then a +40 dB step with `attack_s` near its minimum: the output stayed 40 dB low 400 samples later, where `agc3_cc` stays stable); a rate up to 0.5 is accepted by the validation, so a step of only 3 times the reference could do it. New test `a_huge_step_with_the_fastest_attack_does_not_collapse_the_gain` (`C32` and `CQ15`) |
| 2026-10-25 | M20 | an input sample of exactly zero holds the AGC gain (`C32`: a NaN or zero level does not update the gain; `CQ15`: both raw components zero) | a zero sample carries no information, and without the rule the gain runs up to `max_gain_db` in every pause of a squelched channel (the airband flow `squelch -> agc`), so the next transmission starts at 60 dB and is corrected only by the attack; GNU Radio has no such rule. The `CQ15` rule tests the INPUT, not the quantized output, so a faint signal that quantizes to zero at a low gain still raises the gain (tests `the_gain_never_leaves_its_limits` and `agc_extremes_do_not_panic` use a signal of 1 LSB for the run to the maximum). The tests that expected 'silence drives the gain to the maximum' now expect that the gain is held |
| 2026-10-25 | M20 | the `CQ15` AGC computes the update in `i128` with the rates as Q.48 integers `floor(r * 2^48 + 0.5)` (no division by the reference any more) and a division rounded half up (the integer model and `Reference` use `i128` divisions; `Fallback` computes the same values with 64-bit divisors, which cut the `aarch64` count of `agc_cq15` from 190.78 instructions per sample, with `i128` divisions, to the number in `docs/performance.md`; the test `cq15_fallback_equals_reference_over_parameter_corners` compares both over the corners of the parameters); `lo = ref_raw >> 2`, `hi = ref_raw + (ref_raw >> 1)`; the `C32` constants `lo = reference * 0.25` and `hi = reference * 1.5` are rounded in `f32` | the update has the output level in the denominator, so the loop coefficient no longer carries `1 / reference`; the integer model is in `tests/golden/sdrk_golden/cases/agc.py` (asserts every intermediate below `2^126`) and matches the Rust `Fallback` and `Reference` bit for bit; `C32` uses compare-and-select instead of `f32::max`/`min` (identical for a gain that is never NaN) because the NaN rules of `max`/`min` cost 16 instructions per sample at the baseline level **Superseded on 2026-10-26 for the `CQ15` `Fallback`: it computes the quotients without integer division, from an f64 estimate with one exact 64-bit correction (entry below); `Reference` and the golden model keep the `i128` divisions.** |

With attack 0.1 s and release 0.5 s any AGC that weighs the overshoot more than the undershoot settles below the level of the mean envelope (the pure `agc3_cc` form gives -0.98 dB, so 1 dB is about the best a loop with a ratio of 5 can do), equal rates give +1.2 dB with this update |

| 2026-10-25 | M20 | the `C32` `agc` is judged against the `agc_cc` loop of GNU Radio: a C copy of `agc_cc::scale` and `scaleN` (`gr-analog/include/gnuradio/analog/agc.h`, GNU Radio main at `ec1bf15`) in `benches/refs/gr-agc.c`, built by `benches/refs/build.sh` for every platform with the generic flags of GNU Radio's own build (`-O3`, no `-march`; `-msimd128` on wasm32) and linked into the measurement binary as `libgragc.a` (`cref_sys::GrAgcCc`, `Impl::GnuRadio`, `Reference::GnuRadio`, `Family::GnuRadio` in `xtask`); the entries of `agc` in `benches/limits.json` are `release` (limit 1.10x, unchanged) at `sse2`, `sse4.2`, `avx2`, `avx512` and `simd128`, `dev` at `neon` (open item M20-6), and stay `info` at `avx2-runtime` (the same code compiled for the baseline against the `avx2` build); `agc` `CQ15` (no counterpart in GNU Radio) and `dc_block_real` stay `info` against the fallback | closes the part of M20-6 that asked for a fair reference. GNU Radio's `agc.h` is header-only code, compiled by the user's build with its own flags and never dispatched by VOLK at run time, so one build with generic flags is the loop that GNU Radio runs at every level (Linux distributions build for generic x86-64 and AArch64). Measured with the faithful translation (the four members in one object that the output stores may alias, so the gain is reloaded and stored in every iteration; `sqrtf` with its `errno` rule; `_max_gain > 0.0` as a double comparison, as in the source): 24.03 instructions per sample on x86-64, 17.03 on aarch64 (GCC fuses `a * b + c` into `fmadd` and needs no `errno` branch), 64.05 fuel on wasm32. The 22.0 (x86-64) and 18.0 (`avx2`) of the first entry of M20-6 came from a scratch copy that compared the maximum gain as a float and was compiled with `-mavx2` for the `avx2` figure; they are replaced. For information the faithful loop compiled by GCC with `-mavx2` needs 20.0 (callgrind over 4096 samples), against 20.84 of ours (1.04x). The accuracy column compares each side with the f64 model of its own loop (123.1 dB ours, 118.9 dB GNU Radio) because the two are different algorithms **Superseded on 2026-10-26 in its status and figures: `neon` is `release` and M20-6 is closed, `avx2-runtime` is judged against `avx2` with the cap 1.02x, ours is 119.9 dB (entries below).** |
| 2026-10-25 | M20 | the `C32` `agc` loop of the fallback runs four samples per iteration and tests the direction first: `if level > reference { attack } else if level > 0 { release } else { hold }`, with the clamps as compare-and-select (`next > g_min`, `next < g_max`); the values are bit-identical with the loop before (the test `level > reference` is exactly `reference - level < 0`; the reference is positive, so a level above it is above zero; a NaN level fails both tests and holds the gain) | the loop is a recursion on the gain and cannot be vectorized, so the instruction count is what remains: four samples share the counter, the end test and the branch of the loop (callgrind on the isolated loop: 26.56 to 24.31 at the `x86-64` baseline for the unrolling, 23.58 with the direction test and the select clamps; eight samples measured 26.91 at the baseline, the compiler spills; one and two samples 26.56 and 24.44), and the zero test no longer costs an instruction on the attack path. Measured in the benchmark: 26.48 to 23.84 at `sse2` and `sse4.2`, 23.25 to 20.84 at `avx2` and `avx512`, 25.25 to 21.84 at `neon`. `f32::min`/`max` (`fminnm` on NEON, `f32.min` on wasm32) were measured worse than the compare and select (22.75 against 21.84 and 89.98 against 62.21), so the select form stays. The f64 model of the specification (`Reference`, the golden model) is unchanged, so are the golden vectors (`generate.py --check` passes); `m` is zero where the squares of the input underflow (below about `2^-75`), before where those of the output did |
| 2026-10-26 | M20 | the `C32` release of `agc` is evaluated per range of the level: `g + (r_r ref / |x| - r_r g)` for `lo <= m <= ref` and `g + g ((r_r / lo) (ref - m))` for `0 < m < lo`, with `r_r ref` and `r_r / lo` rounded once to f32 from f64; the attack keeps its form; the zero/NaN test of the hold moves into the branch below `lo` | the same function as `g (d + r_r e) / d` with `d = max(m, lo)` (for `d = m`, `g / m = 1 / |x|`), with no division by the level in the main release and none at all below `lo`, and the hold test only where a zero or NaN level can arrive. Both forms add the update to `g` last: the form `(1 - r_r) g + r_r ref / |x|` would save one instruction but rounds `1 - r_r` to f32 once, which changes the effective rate by up to `2^-25 / r_r` relative; it gave 103 dB against 123 dB and was not taken. Accuracy now 119.9 dB on the bench input (accuracy rule: 118.9 of GNU Radio), 125.5 dB on `steps_c32_24000` (contract 80 dB) |

A loop of eight steps is not unrolled by the compiler (22.01). The `unsafe` rule of the backend (helpers that the safe intrinsics cannot express) is extended by this case in `level.rs`. No FMA (D5) |

| 2026-10-26 | M20 | the `C32` gain is clamped to `[g_min, g_max]` on both sides in the attack and in the release form `g + (c / |x| - r_r g)`, on the side of `g_max` only in the form `g + g ((r_r / lo) (ref - m))` | review finding: with a clamp on one side (the attack to `g_min`, the release to `g_max`) a gain at a limit could leave it by one rounding near `m = ref` (the release form rounds below the old gain where its update is a rounding below zero, the attack `g d / den` above it where `den` rounds to `d`); a search found 289 cases among 4.8 million probes at `g_min` and 52 510 among 8.6 million at `g_max` (for example reference 0.0809, release rate 0.5, `min = initial = -25.3` dB and one sample of magnitude 1.4897274: the gain went from 5.4325033e-2 to 5.432503e-2, -25.3000006 dB). This broke acceptance test 4 of the milestone (the gain never leaves `[min, max]`) and the clamp of section 3; the test `c32_gain_at_a_limit_stays_there_exactly` holds both cases without tolerance. The last form adds `g` times a product of non-negative factors to `g` and never lowers it, so its clamp to `g_min` would change no value. Cost: one `fmaxnm`/`fminnm` per sample at `neon` (17.03 to 17.61, 1.00x to 1.03x of `agc_cc`), a compare and a select on wasm32 (61.08 to 64.61 fuel, 0.95x to 1.01x), 0.48 to 0.64 instructions per sample on x86-64; all within the release limit of 1.10x. The float32 model of `cases/agc.py` now follows the arithmetic of the fallback and gives its values bit for bit (125.5 dB against the f64 model); the golden vectors (from the f64 model) are unchanged |
| 2026-10-26 | M20 | the `CQ15` `agc` fallback computes the quotients of the update without integer division: an f64 estimate of the quotient plus `1/2` gives a candidate `k`, and the sign of the remainder `n - k * divisor` (exact as the wrapping difference of the low 64 bits, because the bounds of the domain keep it below `2^63` in magnitude: below `0.55 * 0.854 * 2^64` for the attack, `0.6 * 2^63` for the release) picks `k` or `k - 1`; the rounded square root is the nearest integer to one f64 square root (exact for `s <= 2^32`, `num::round_sqrt_u32`); on x86-64 the f64 values become integers by adding `2^52`, on AArch64 and wasm32 by `as` (`num::near_int_f64`, `near_int_plus_half_f64`); the round-half-up shift of the output skips the saturation of `round_shift` (no overflow below `2^62`) and the gain limit is `max`/`min` instead of `clamp` | the `u128` division of the attack is the software `__udivti3` (620.79 fuel per sample on wasm32); with the change 141.14 fuel, and 72.86 / 72.86 / 69.40 / 70.31 / 54.77 instructions per sample at `sse2` / `sse4.2` / `avx2` / `avx512` / `neon` instead of 124.61 / 124.61 / 125.08 / 110.27 / 111.35; the `2^52` conversion was measured on every platform and helps only x86-64 (57.50 instead of 54.77 at `neon`, 142.60 instead of 141.14 fuel); `Reference` and the golden model keep the `i128` divisions, and the values are unchanged bit for bit (unit tests against `u128` division at the domain edges, at 2 million random points and next to multiples of the divisor, run natively, under QEMU for AArch64 and under wasmtime) |

The figures per target, before the review (c45da86) / the review with `black_box` (a7b92f7) / now: `sse2` 23.84 / 27.87 / 28.24, `avx2` 20.84 / 22.73 / 23.36, `neon` 21.84 / 22.92 / 22.42, `simd128` 62.21 / 66.89 / 64.11 fuel, Cortex-M4 80.50 / 75.52 / 76.16. | 2026-10-28 | M22 | D26 (implementer decision, open item M22-9): two changes to the block of sixteen samples of the `f32` pair in `fallback/biquad.rs`, both bit-exact (every operation and its order unchanged, D17 and D20 kept, no FMA, no target-specific code): the flush of the second section B, due after its sample 14, moves to between the step of the first section A and the step of B of sample 15, beside the flush of A (the states are independent, so this is the same point of B's schedule); in the other fifteen samples B writes its output before its state update (`step_store`), so the output register is free for the last product of the update and SSE needs one register copy less per sample. | 2026-10-28 | M22 | D28 (implementer decision, follows from D27): the `Q15`/`CQ15` cascade of the fallback has a second form for targets with 32-bit integer registers (`target_pointer_width = "32"` and not wasm32, which has 64-bit integer operations; the precedent is the integer `convert_out` path of bare-metal 32-bit Arm and RISC-V): the state in `i32` instead of `i64`, each product `i64::from(a) * i64::from(b)` of two `i32` (one `smlal` on the M4 instead of a full 64 x 64 bit product), the feedback coefficients negated once so that the sum is one chain, the clamp in `i32` after the shift (`ssat`; the shifted sum is below 2^20 in magnitude), and the two components of `CQ15` in two passes, so that the state of one component stays in registers. The output is the same bit for bit (all arithmetic is exact; unit test `the_narrow_q15_cascade_equals_the_wide_one` compares both forms on the host over the Q2.14 extremes, random and unstable coefficients, full-scale input and split calls; the kernel tests of the M4 suite run the new form). The 64-bit targets and wasm32 keep the `i64` form unchanged | measured on the Cortex-M4 (`CQ15` / `Q15`, instructions per sample): `i64` form 326.06 / 103.78; `i32` state with the clamp after the shift 104.04 / 41.78; with the negated feedback coefficients 83.54 / 33.03; with the two passes for `CQ15` 73.29 / 33.53 (taken: the passes are in a helper shared by both types; `Q15` written with its own loops measured 36.27). The `i32` form on every target, measured and dropped: `CQ15` 119.05 / 119.05 / 104.06 / 94.06 / 85.04 / 262.10 at `sse2` / `sse4.2` / `avx2` / `avx512` / `neon` / `simd128` against 110.05 / 110.05 / 130.05 / 101.05 / 66.04 / 199.09 with `i64`, `Q15` 57.04 / 57.04 / 48.04 / 46.04 / 38.03 / 126.08 against 57.04 / 57.04 / 48.54 / 48.04 / 34.03 / 110.07: the widening of every operand costs more on `neon` and `simd128` than it saves elsewhere. No limit changed: the host entries are those of before **Note 2026-10-28 (D30): the Cortex-M4 figures above were taken with the generic Armv7E-M tuning; with `-C target-cpu=cortex-m4` the `i64` form counts 322.06 / 103.28 and the taken `i32` form 82.05 / 37.78 (`CQ15` / `Q15`), so the choice stands; the intermediate variants were not re-measured.** |

| 2026-10-28 | M22 | D29 (implementer decision, open item M22-8): KFR 7.2.0 is built for wasm32 and gates `biquad` C32 and `biquad_real` f32 at `simd128` as on the other levels, CMSIS-DSP's generic C becomes information there. KFR has no wasm32 code of its own; its CMake build for Emscripten sets `KFR_ARCH=sse41` and compiles with `-msse4.1 -msimd128`. `benches/refs/build.sh wasm32` repeats exactly that with the system clang (`clang++ --target=wasm32-wasip1 -std=c++20 -fno-exceptions -fno-rtti -fno-threadsafe-statics -O3 -ffp-contract=off -msimd128`): the macros emcc defines for `-msse4.1` (`__SSE__`, `__SSE2__`, `__SSE3__`, `__SSSE3__`, `__SSE4_1__`), the `*intrin.h` headers of Emscripten 6.0.12 (SSE written with SIMD128 intrinsics) and the libc++ and wasi-libc headers of the wasi-sdk 34 sysroot, both releases current when KFR 7.2.0 came out (2026-10-12), downloaded once and checked against their SHA-256 like the other tarballs. The shim needs no library of either (its only imports are `aligned_alloc` and `free`, which the Rust target's wasi-libc provides), so the count binary still links with the Rust toolchain alone and runs in wasmtime with fuel. No new tool: `clang++` is part of the `clang` package that the dev container and the count job already install. Measured in a full `cargo xtask count --platform wasm32` run: `biquad_real` 40.09 against KFR 48.39 (0.83; CMSIS-DSP 57.94, 0.69, information), `biquad` C32 43.04 against KFR 96.77 (0.44; CMSIS-DSP 118.83, 0.36, information); both `release` and `pass`, KFR's SNR equals ours (136.72 and 136.06 dB); every other record of `benches/results/wasm32.json` is unchanged, `references` lists KFR, the sysroot and Emscripten. | KFR's SSE emulation is no strawman: KFR's filter is written with clang's vector extensions, which clang compiles to SIMD128 directly (the shim's code has 65 `f32x4.mul` and 52 `f32x4.add`/`sub`, the emulated SSE intrinsics are a few shuffles), and it is the configuration KFR itself ships for the web. It is the stronger reference at `simd128`: it counts 16 % (`f32`) and 19 % (`C32`) fewer instructions than CMSIS-DSP's generic C, so the entries got stricter (0.83 and 0.44 instead of 0.69 and 0.36) and still pass. A pinned emsdk would bring its own LLVM, binaryen and Node.js (several hundred MB) and a second compiler next to the one of the other wasm32 references, for the same headers and the same KFR configuration. KFR's generic architecture (no SSE macros) is no alternative: it does not compile in 7.2.0 (`bitshuffle.hpp` names `intr::bitpermute_pair`, which only the x86 and ARM code define) |

| 2026-10-28 | issue #20 | D32 (maintainer): comparison policy for the counts. Every kernel gets an optimal branch at every SIMD level (`sse2`, `sse4.2`, `avx2`, `avx512`, `neon`, `simd128`) and an efficient fallback. A SIMD branch is compared with an established, well-tested library (VOLK, KFR, CMSIS-DSP, FFTW, IPP, ...) at the same level, with the same algorithm class and the same work; the fallback with established scalar code (CMSIS-DSP generic C, the loops of GNU Radio). A SIMD branch against our own fallback is at most `info`, never `release` or `dev`. Without a fair reference the branch is judged by its efficiency (D33) instead of "about as efficient as our other SIMD branches" ([performance.md](performance.md#what-is-compared-with-what-d32)) | only a fair external comparison shows whether a branch is good; a SIMD branch beats a scalar loop almost always, so entries gated against the fallback were green without proving anything. The entries that do not follow the policy yet are listed in issue #20 (groups A to D) |
| 2026-10-28 | issue #20 | D33 (maintainer): speed is judged per instruction-set level by cycles per sample simulated with `llvm-mca` (pinned LLVM; 23.1.1, the LLVM of the Rust toolchain at the time, built from source in the development container), not only by instructions per sample. One reference core model and one counter-model of another vendor or core generation per level (`sse2`/`sse4.2`: `skylake` / `znver2`; `avx2`: `znver3` / `alderlake`; `avx512` built for `x86-64-v4`: `znver4` / `sapphirerapids`; `neon`: `cortex-a72` / `cortex-a76`, which LLVM 23.1.1 simulates with the models of `cortex-a57` and `neoverse-n1`; in LLVM 21 both used the `cortex-a57` model); only the ratio ours / reference with the same tool and model is used; the hot basic blocks come from the execution counts of the existing counters; the efficiency `port bound / simulated cycles` with a bottleneck class judges branches without a reference. No real hardware, no calibration, no published absolute cycles or MS/s, no vendor- or model-specific branches. Instructions per sample stay the gate on in-order cores (Cortex-M, ESP32) and WASM and become a regression guard on x86-64 and AArch64. The thresholds (≤ 1.00 goal on the reference model, at most about 5 % worse on the counter-model) are proposed and not yet in `benches/limits.json`; until the tooling exists, instructions per sample stay the gate everywhere ([performance.md](performance.md#speed-simulated-cycles-per-sample-level-2)) | instructions measure work, not speed (`cycles = instructions / IPC`, IPC about 0.3 to 5); optimizations for out-of-order cores such as several accumulators, unrolling and the wavefront of D18 add instructions and make the code faster, so a gate on instructions penalizes them. One `f32` biquad section: 15 instructions, 10.0 simulated cycles on Zen 4, port bound 2.5 cycles. A reduced model set instead of a matrix of about 15 models and three tools, because the goal is an efficient branch per level, not per CPU |
| 2026-10-28 | issue #20 | D34 (maintainer): accuracy policy. The f64 golden is the mathematical optimum and the first target of every kernel, float and Q15 alike: the SNR against it is judged against the achievable optimum (the f64 result rounded once to the output type; a target, not a gate). Only where that optimum cannot be reached by principle or data type is our distance from it compared with an established implementation of the same algorithm against the same golden (`count accuracy`); "at most 1 dB below the comparison" is a floor, not the target. Every tolerance case additionally gets a regression guard (the lowest SNR measured over the levels and builds minus 1 dB; not for the relaxed WASM build) next to its contract floor, and Q15 kernels get an SNR check against an f64 golden next to their bit-exact case ([kernels.md](kernels.md#accuracy-policy)) | an established library can be inaccurate itself (speed-for-accuracy trade-offs, bugs), so it is a yardstick, never the target; the contract floors are loose (`biquad` met 90 to 100 dB at about 135 dB, and only the comparison with KFR and CMSIS-DSP showed the 1.1 to 1.2 dB that D20 recovered), and bit-exact Q15 goldens prove agreement with our own model, not closeness to the optimum |
| 2026-10-28 | issue #20 | D35 (implementer decision, follows D33): the simulated cycles are computed in xtask (`xtask/src/mca.rs`, run by `cargo measure`) instead of a `count mca` command of the measurement binary: callgrind records the execution count of every instruction (`--dump-instr=yes`) of a run with the counted calls and of one without them, the difference is cut into basic blocks (equal counts, ended by every control transfer), `objdump` disassembles them from the object files, and `llvm-mca` simulates the blocks that cover 99 % of the executed instructions (at most 64) as loops of 200 iterations; weighted by their counts they give cycles and port bound per sample, the rest of the instructions gets the cycles per instruction of the simulated ones. The bottleneck class is `throughput` from an efficiency of 0.8 up, `latency` below. Only levels counted with callgrind are simulated (`avx2`); the results are reported, not judged, and not written to `benches/results` | the counts per instruction come from the counter that already measures the level, so the profiled code is exactly the counted code, the references included, without markers in their sources; callgrind reports addresses relative to each object file, so `objdump` disassembles the hot blocks directly. SDE (`-dcfg`) and the QEMU plug-ins can give the same counts for the other levels; that is the next step. Basic blocks with the same count that are adjacent apart from code that never ran (at most 256 bytes) are joined into one loop body: simulated one by one, the blocks of a loop with a saturation compiled as a jump each became a loop of their own and lost the chain through them (the first version without this gave the Q15 biquad fallback 16.88 instead of 10.84 cycles per sample on `znver3`). First results at `avx2` (ours / reference, simulated cycles on `znver3` / `alderlake`, instruction ratio in brackets): `biquad_real` against KFR 0.82 / 0.86 (0.49), `biquad_real_fallback` against CMSIS-DSP 0.56 / 0.59 (0.95), `biquad_real_q15_fallback` against CMSIS-DSP 0.69 / 1.42 (0.67; uncertain: the `znver3` model gives 10.6 cycles per loop iteration, below the 18 cycles of its loop-carried dependency that OSACA finds on ZEN3 and SPR and `llvm-mca` on `alderlake`), `biquad_real_q15` against our fallback 1.97 / 1.82 (0.39), `biquad_cq15` against our fallback 0.69 / 1.17 (0.16), `biquad_cq15_fallback` against CMSIS-DSP 0.80 / 0.99 (0.90) |
| 2026-10-28 | issue #20 | D36 (implementer decision): the `Q15`/`CQ15` biquad fallback for 64-bit targets and wasm32 (`q15_wide`) carries two partial sums per component, `s = b1 x1 + b2 x2 - a1 y1 - a2 y2 + 2^13` and `q = b2 x1 - a2 y1 + 2^13`, so that `y = sat16((b0 x + s) >> 14)`, `s' = (b1 x + q) - a1 y` and `q' = b2 x - a2 y + 2^13`; the `Q15` sections run in pairs (each sample through both, as the `f32` fallback does), `CQ15` stays one section per pass (its two components are already two chains); the history `x1, x2, y1, y2` is moved only by the steps of the last two samples of a buffer. The output is the same bit for bit (every sum is exact in `i64`, only the output is rounded); the 32-bit form of D28 is unchanged | found with `cargo measure` (D35): the chain from one output to the next was `imul`, `add`, `sub`, the addition of the rounding constant and the shift, because the compiler groups `a1 y1 + a2 y2` first in one expression; it does not regroup across loop iterations, so the carried sums keep the chain at `imul`, `sub`, `add`, shift (OSACA on ZEN3 and SPR: 7 cycles per sample through both sections, against 18 per sample before, with two sections one after the other). Instructions per sample, before / after: `biquad_real_q15_fallback` 57.04 / 46.05 (`sse2`, `sse4.2`), 48.54 / 46.05 (`avx2`), 48.04 / 45.05 (`avx512`), 34.03 / 26.05 (`neon`), 110.07 / 96.08 (`simd128`); `biquad_cq15_fallback` 110.05 / 100.07, 130.05 / 104.07, 101.05 / 99.08, 66.04 / 58.06, 199.09 / 203.11 (`simd128` 2 % more: the carried sums cost one addition per component, the moves of the history saved the rest of the first version's 13 %). Simulated cycles at `avx2` (`znver3` / `alderlake`): `Q15` 10.84 / 18.08 before, 11.11 / 12.12 after (the `znver3` model stayed below the loop-carried dependency of the old loop, see D35), `CQ15` 25.23 / 25.27 before, 21.19 / 21.26 after. Moving the rounding constant in one expression does nothing (the compiler restores its grouping), carrying only `s` made the chain longer (it grouped `a2 y1 + a1 y`) |
| 2026-10-28 | issue #20 | D37 (maintainer): working rule for optimizing until `cargo measure` simulates the cycles of every level. Code shared by all levels (the portable fallback, generic `fearless_simd` code) is optimized by the simulated cycles at `avx2` (`znver3`, `alderlake`); the other levels are checked by their instruction counts, which stay the gate, and by a cycle check when their instructions rise against the trend and as a sample before a kernel change is merged. Code of one level (a hand-written SIMD path) is optimized by the simulated cycles of that level. `simd128` and the in-order cores are optimized by instructions ([performance.md](performance.md#working-rule-for-optimizing-d37-d68)) | only `avx2` is simulated by `cargo measure` today (callgrind); a proof of concept outside the repository simulated the `Q15` biquad fallback of D36 at the other levels too (execution counts per basic block from SDE `-dcfg` for `sse2`, `sse4.2` and `avx512`, from a QEMU plug-in for `neon`; the instructions per sample from these counts equal the counted ones). Before / after in cycles per sample: `sse2` and `sse4.2` `skylake` / `znver2` 18.16 / 20.17 to 15.10 / 15.14 (`Q15`), 29.19 / 34.18 to 24.40 / 31.18 (`CQ15`); `avx512` `znver4` / `sapphirerapids` 10.50 / 18.08 to 14.56 / 13.07 (`Q15`; OSACA on ZEN4 8.0 to 6.4, so uncertain), 22.19 / 26.18 to 21.51 / 20.70 (`CQ15`); `neon` `cortex-a72` / `cortex-a76` 31.12 / 32.13 to 15.10 / 28.09 (`Q15`), 24.19 / 56.08 to 24.13 / 38.12 (`CQ15`). The gain at `avx2` carried over in 13 of 16 comparisons: a guide for shared code, not a proof, and no statement about code of another level |
| 2026-10-29 | issue #20 | D38 (implementer decision): `convert_out`. The AVX2 `I16`/`I8` paths take the NaN mask from the input (`vcmpordps` beside the product), cap with `vminps(scale, v)`, which keeps NaN, and AND the converted integers: one load more per vector (instructions 0.80 / 0.84 to 0.92 / 0.97 of VOLK) but off the dependency chain. | 2026-10-29 | issue #20 | D56 (implementer decision): `convert_out` fallback. The formats with one value per byte or 16-bit word (`U8`, `I8`, `I16`, either byte order, interleaved or real, full scale a power of two) use a branch-free `f32` loop: NaN cleared, clamp, rounding by adding `1.5 * 2^23`; `U8` takes `floor` of `x * 2^7 + 2^-47` with the even neighbour at integers. Bit-exact with the previous `f64` formula for every `f32` (exhaustive ignored test). The clamp is `f32::max`/`min` on x86 and AArch64 and a NaN-free form elsewhere; bare-metal Arm and RISC-V keep the integer path. The SIMD `q15_blocks` cuts input and output to their common length (fixes the placement of the vectors after the main loop for unequal lengths). | 2026-10-31 | issue #20 | D64 (implementer decision): `cargo measure` simulates the CET markers `endbr64` and `endbr32` as `nop` on every model (`xtask/src/mca.rs`, `cet_markers_as_nop`, applied in `simulate_block`, so in both cycle modes and in both forms of the register-move check); the instruction counts keep them, the `notrack` prefix stays (modelled as the plain jump). Ours: unchanged except where it calls glibc's `memmove`: `fir_decimate` `C32` 2.42 / 2.40, `fir_decimate` `CQ15` 29.34 / 24.00 (`znver3`; `alderlake` 19.93 / 19.85). Figures on main at 91d2b02 (golden SNR / optimum / contract floor in dB): `rotate` C32 144.9 to 145.2 / 151.9 to 152.0 / 120; `rotate` CQ15 89.3 to 93.9 / 90.0 to 98.2 / 80; `fm_mod` C32 133.2 / 153.7 / 120; `fm_mod` CQ15 89.6 / 98.0 to 98.1 / 80; `f32` FFT, `dot`, `fir` 138 to 143 / about 152 / 110 to 120. Speed: every variant is well below its reference (avx2 whole-loop cycles, alderlake: `rotate` C32 SIMD 0.19 against VOLK rotator2, fallback 0.40 against GNU Radio; `fm_mod` C32 SIMD 0.08 and fallback 0.25 against GNU Radio frequency_modulator_fc; the CQ15 fallbacks 0.18 and 0.29 against CMSIS-DSP). Reason: 133 dB and more lie far above what any following stage can resolve (16-bit DAC about 98 dB, real 24-bit converters about 120 to 130 dB), and every following `f32` kernel of a chain adds an error of the same order (FIR about 140 dB, FFT about 138 dB), so a closer approach to the optimum would not change the result of a chain. A cost-free improvement (table values optimized for linear interpolation, estimated 3 to 6 dB) is rejected as well, because it would change the bits of `rotate`, `fm_mod`, `ddc`, `duc` and the e2e chains for no practical gain. The regression guards stay (golden SNR minus 1 dB), so these kernels may not get less accurate; the decision is revisited only if a use case needs more. |
| 2026-10-31 | issue #20 | D67 (maintainer decision of 2026-10-31): a narrow exception for exactly two square roots, not a general permission. The optional, default-off feature `std` of `sdrk-core` may do one thing only: switch `num::sqrt_f32` / `num::sqrt_f64` from `libm` to `f32::sqrt` / `f64::sqrt`, with bit-identical results; `no_std` and embedded builds are unchanged. Any future use of `std` in `sdrk-core`, and any similar case (a feature that changes which implementation a core function uses for speed), needs its own systemic decision by the maintainer and is not covered by this row. `cargo xtask check` enforces it: in `sdrk-core` every `std` token, `feature = "std"` and inherent `sqrt` call outside the bodies of these two functions in `num.rs` and the `extern crate std` under `#[cfg(feature = "std")]` in `lib.rs` is a finding (`xtask/src/scan.rs`, `scan_core_std`). Gain: `mag_fallback` at `avx2` 5.15 / 1.71 -> 0.90 / 0.38 cycles per sample (`znver3` / `alderlake`, whole-loop). Rejected alternatives: `unsafe` `core::arch` calls (break the `forbid(unsafe_code)` of the core); waiting for `core::f32::math::sqrt` (unstable, `core_float_math`; once it is stable it replaces the feature, which is then retired). Details: `sdrk-core` gets the feature `std` (off by default; enabled by `sdrk/std`, `sdrk-backend-simd/std` and, on every level, by the measurement binary `benches/kernels`). With it `num::sqrt_f32` / `sqrt_f64` are `f32::sqrt` / `f64::sqrt`, LLVM's `llvm.sqrt`, which the compiler vectorizes; without it they stay `libm` (lane 0 of `f32x4_sqrt` / `f64x2_sqrt` on wasm32 with `simd128`). The heap check gives the core layer the policy of the backends (`extern crate std` under `#[cfg(feature = "std")]`, no `std::` path but `std::arch`); the inherent `f32::sqrt` needs no path. The tail of the SIMD `agc` magnitudes keeps `libm::sqrtf` outside wasm32 (`tail_sqrt`). `mag_fallback` is `release` on every level (the waiver below `simd128` is dropped) | IEEE 754 rounds the square root correctly, so every conforming implementation gives the same bits; tested in `num::tests` against `libm` and `std` over every 7th subnormal, every 251st positive finite pattern, NaN payloads, negative patterns, zeros and infinities (f32) and a Weyl walk over all f64 patterns, through the slice loop that vectorizes (debug and `--release`); goldens unchanged. On stable Rust there is no other safe way: `core::f32::math::sqrt` is unstable (`core_float_math`), and the `core::arch` intrinsics of x86 and AArch64 (`_mm_sqrt_ss`, `vsqrt_f32`) are `#[target_feature]` functions that need `unsafe` outside such a function, which `forbid(unsafe_code)` of the core excludes; `libm` writes `sqrtss` / `fsqrt` as inline assembly that LLVM cannot vectorize. A vectorized `agc` tail (fewer than two vectors, last block only) cost spills in the recursion inlined into the same function: `avx2` `znver3` / `alderlake` whole-loop 13.18 / 14.47 -> 14.22 / 16.24 cycles per sample with `sqrt_f32`, back to 13.18 / 14.47 with `libm::sqrtf`. Measured (`avx2`, whole-loop, `znver3` / `alderlake`, cycles per sample, before -> after, CMSIS-DSP 1.18.0 generic C in brackets): `mag_fallback` 5.15 / 1.71 -> 0.90 / 0.38 (1.15 / 0.59), `mag_q15_fallback` 7.93 / 10.05 -> 1.93 / 1.28 (129.29 / 83.53); instructions per sample `mag_fallback` 10.01 / 9.01 / 9.01 / 9.01 / 7.01 / 8.28 -> 3.26 / 3.26 / 1.76 / 1.23 / 2.01 / 8.28 (`sse2` / `sse4.2` / `avx2` / `avx512` / `neon` / `simd128`; CMSIS-DSP 4.27 / 4.27 / 2.40 / 1.89 / 3.01 / 10.91), `mag_q15_fallback` 39.01 / 34.01 / 35.01 / 33.01 / 28.01 / 12.90 -> 9.76 / 6.76 / 3.64 / 1.83 / 4.64 / 12.90; `agc`, `agc_fallback`, `mag` unchanged. Spot checks: `sse2` `skylake` / `znver2` per block `mag_fallback` 0.80 / 1.80 against CMSIS-DSP 0.85 / 1.86; `neon` `cortex-a72` / `cortex-a76` 8.56 / 1.77 against 8.82 / 2.04, `mag_q15_fallback` 9.94 / 4.07. Rejected: `unsafe` `core::arch` calls under `cfg(target_feature)` (would also serve `no_std` builds on x86-64 and AArch64, but breaks the `forbid(unsafe_code)` rule of the non-backend crates, M00); Cortex-M4 and RISC-V keep `libm` (no `std`; `vsqrt.f32` / `fsqrt.s` would need `asm!`) |
| 2026-10-31 | issue #20 | D68 (maintainer decision of 2026-10-31): the speed target is the simulated cycles per sample on every level with a core model: the scalar fallbacks and `sse2`, `sse4.2`, `avx2`, `avx512` and `neon`. Instructions per sample are only the gate for gross outliers there. Execution counts from emulators (Intel SDE, QEMU) plus `llvm-mca` are sufficient: ours and its reference run with the same variant and optimization in the same emulator on the same emulated CPU (D32), so the measurement is sufficient in relative terms; absolute values may deviate through the inaccuracy of the emulators and the models and do not matter. Only the ratio ours / reference with the same tool, the same model and the same counter counts; absolute cycles are not presented as hardware figures. `simd128` has no core model and stays gated by instructions; its cycles would be informational at most (the JIT of the engine decides the machine code) and are not simulated. The cycles stay report-only: no cycle gates and no change of the statuses of `benches/limits.json` (the gates are a separate decision of the maintainer). Implementation (`cargo measure`, `xtask/src/blockcount.rs`): `sse2` and `sse4.2` take the block and edge counts of SDE's `-dcfg` with the CPUID definition of the count (`-cpuid_in`, `-chip_check_cpuid 1`), `avx512` with `-spr`, both between the SSC markers of the count; `neon` takes the translation-block and transfer counts of the plugin `xtask/plugins/bbcount.c` under the `qemu-aarch64` of the count (`-R`, the sysroot, the library path), which xtask builds against the `qemu-plugin.h` of the pinned QEMU 10.2.1; the plugin maps every block to its object and file address through the file mappings of the QEMU process, so VOLK's shared library is counted with its load base; the same binaries, `volk_config` pin and references as the count. The FFT entries have the restore of the input subtracted as on callgrind; executions a subtraction leaves negative (the harness code of the restore-only run) are deducted from the total, so that the total is the count (`fft_64` at `neon` read 10.52 against the count's 10.42 without this). An entry that cannot be simulated gets a `note`, never a zero. Validation: (a) 12 `avx2` entries (`fft_1024`, `fft_1024_fallback`, the four `mag_sq` and the four `dot_real` entries, `rotate_cq15`, `rotate_cq15_fallback`; VOLK references among them) counted under SDE `-hsw` (`--cycle-counter sde`) give the instructions per sample of the count on both sides and the cycles of callgrind on `znver3` and `alderlake` to the last digit, except VOLK's `dot_real` (+0.1 %); (b) at `sse2`, `sse4.2`, `avx512` and `neon` the instructions per sample implied by the block counts agree with `benches/results` to two decimals for all 123 entries and both sides (246 comparisons per level, no exception). All 123 entries are simulated on every level. At `avx2` (callgrind, unchanged) four VOLK sides differ from `benches/results` in the second decimal: `convert_out_cs16` 2.1450 / 2.15 and `power_db` 4.8050 / 4.81 (rounding at the half), `convert_out_real_ru8` 1.1148 / 1.12, `rotate` 5.2561 / 5.28 | before, only `avx2` had execution counts per instruction (callgrind), so shared code was steered by `avx2` cycles alone (D37) and a hand-written path of another level by a proof of concept outside the repository; the instruction counts of the out-of-order levels did not show speed (several accumulators, unrolling and wavefronts add instructions). The emulators already give the exact counts of the gate, and the fair comparison in one emulator cancels most of their weaknesses |
